# Load and frame-time audit — October 2, 2026

- Codex built this audit on `codex/perf-audit`.
- Baseline: main at `4e55c9f6d32a`.
- Coverage: 319 public and 23 labs toys; 684/684 setting rows.
- The settings run on the Mac GPU; physical-phone behavior is unverified.
- Largest opening payload: `video-3d`, 12.84 MiB.
- Slowest first-ready frame: `gaussian-splatting`, 972.20 ms.
- The low tier already uses lite captures; their counts have no generator cap.
- Unused PLY columns: 1.33 MiB less; decoded scene data matched.
- Other quality cuts need visual comparison; raw samples accompany the tables.

## Every toy, worst opening payload first

MiB = 1,048,576 bytes. Frame pairs are median / p95 in milliseconds. The raw data preserves
unrounded values. Phone uses the weak tier on Mac Chrome; desktop uses high. First frame is the
first ready submitted render; frame pairs are frameend cadence, including update-only loops. See the
method below for those limits and the opening cutoff.

## The 25 heaviest toys on each measure

Public and labs toys are ranked together here; the full inventories above remain separate. Each
setting has its own top 25. Ties use toy ID. Small timing differences within the refresh/GC/noise
band should not determine a quality cut. Source explanations below are candidates, not CPU/GPU
profiling proof.

## Toy-by-toy changes worth testing

These are proposals, not edits or verified savings in frame time. Start with lossless reuse and
deferred work. A lower splat count is a trial that can make a solid surface grainy or open gaps; no
row claims it would look the same. The 25% trials below estimate only count and geometry-array
storage, not the observed JS heap. A kit’s position, scale, quaternion, color, and animation arrays
total 72 bytes per allocated splat (`src/generators.js:96`); clay slack, extra streams, worker and
GPU copies are additional. Kit sampling scales with the requested budget (`src/kit.js:854`) but
rounding and minimums can change the final count. Counts already include those differences.

Three temporary decoded normal columns also total 1.33 MiB. Live gzip saving needs a new measurement.                                                                                         | Decoded fields and the framed/pruned scene matched exactly in the temporary trial. Visual and external PLY-consumer acceptance remain untested; no repository asset was edited.         | `src/packs/video3d.js:293`            |

The seven Real objects files contain 258,505–275,920 points, with a 44-byte ROS1 header and 14 bytes
per point (`src/packs/real-objects.js:30`). Yet addScan uses an initial prefix bounded by the kit
budget and a share below one (`src/packs/real-objects.js:71`). A low-only prefix capped at 120,000
points would cover the requested low budget; preserving the sigma-growth product requires shifting
the encoded log-sigma range by half the log of the old/new count ratio. This is a candidate for
preserving the current phone geometry while reducing downloads and 41-byte-per-point decoded arrays.
It needs point-by-point comparison and visual review before use; no files were trimmed in this
audit.

## Site-wide load and deferred libraries

The gallery creates all cards up front but marks thumbnails lazy (`src/ui.js:214`, `src/ui.js:227`).
The complete thumbnail inventory is 2,676,612 bytes for public toys and 313,690 bytes for labs toys.
The visible lazy subset varies with selected-card scrolling; it is not valid to subtract a universal
gallery constant from each toy’s opening payload. Before an explicit visitor choice, the normal page
automatically opens Cactus (`src/app.js:193`), imports PlayCanvas through `src/pc.js:5`, and imports
the UI, physics, effects, voice and sound metadata. Help text is deferred until the first toy opens
(`src/ui.js:2722`); the flag catalog loads when the flag lists are initialized (`src/ui.js:1860`).
The gallery-specific probe and category breakdown are saved separately.

Sound is off in a fresh visit (`src/sound.js:17`); the code preloads recorded samples when a builtin
toy opens only if sound is enabled (`src/app.js:799`, `src/sound.js:65`). Thus “no sound download”
in this matrix applies to the clean default, not a returning sound-enabled visitor. Lazy loading
those samples later could reduce enabled opening bytes but can delay the first note; preserve the
current preload behavior unless that tradeoff is accepted.

PDF.js loads inside openPDF (`src/media.js:278`), and a default PDF sample makes a PDF toy need it
as soon as that toy opens. omggif loads on the GIF fallback path (`src/media.js:426`) and moving-GIF
conversion (`src/packs/moving-photo.js:54`). ONNX Runtime and the depth model load from photo
conversion (`src/packs/photo-3d.js:198`, `src/packs/photo-3d-depth.js:59`) or the live depth worker
(`src/live/depth-worker.js:26`); default precomputed photo/video samples are a different path. The
request census and additional probes distinguish the default page, default toy samples, and the
conversion path. No claim is made that these libraries remain unloaded after a toy actually needs
its default PDF or an explicit conversion.

### Observed gallery/startup payloads

These probes start at the normal Cactus page, wait five seconds, open the gallery, and then force
every available thumbnail to load. The columns are cumulative completed HTTP body bytes, in MiB;
“all thumbnails” is a deliberate exhaustive probe, not the normal lazy visitor path. Public-only
galleries have 319 cards; labs-enabled galleries have 342.

| Setting | Shelf         | Initial Cactus page MiB | After gallery opens MiB | All thumbnails MiB | Thumbnail bodies after all MiB | Loaded cards |
| ------- | ------------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------------ |
| phone   | Public        | 5.47                    | 5.80                    | 7.89               | 2.55                           | 319/319      |
| phone   | Public + labs | 5.47                    | 5.80                    | 8.19               | 2.85                           | 342/342      |
| desktop | Public        | 9.83                    | 11.25                   | 11.95              | 2.55                           | 319/319      |
| desktop | Public + labs | 9.83                    | 11.24                   | 12.24              | 2.85                           | 342/342      |

Before a visitor chooses a toy, the initial public-page request census includes these categories.
CSS/document includes the navigation entry; module totals include help and metadata modules.

| Category                | Phone MiB | Desktop MiB |
| ----------------------- | --------- | ----------- |
| PlayCanvas engine       | 2.35      | 2.35        |
| App/UI/metadata modules | 1.29      | 1.29        |
| Default Cactus asset    | 1.54      | 5.60        |
| Lazy thumbnails         | 0.13      | 0.43        |
| Flag catalog data       | 0.05      | 0.05        |
| Other vendor code       | 0.01      | 0.01        |
| Document/CSS/other      | 0.10      | 0.10        |

A gallery-first landing could defer the default Cactus capture, 1,612,777 weak-tier or 5,869,356
high-tier local body bytes, until a choice; it would remove the immediate interactive preview.
Export-only code is another small deferral candidate: gifenc is already fetched on startup (9,060
local body bytes), through `src/exports.js:5`; it is a separate encoder from the lazy omggif
decoder. A deferred export can make the first export slower. Deferring the 53,129-byte flag catalog
until a flag list is needed (`src/ui.js:1860`) is another startup candidate; the first flag picker
would then wait for its data.

### Deferred-library request census

| Library      | Default toy/setting cells with a request | Toys                       |
| ------------ | ---------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
| PDF.js       | 4                                        | `picture-lab`, `your-book` |
| omggif       | 0                                        | None                       |
| ONNX Runtime | 0                                        | None                       |
| Depth model  | 0                                        | None                       |

All four normal-page/gallery probes have zero requests for these four library/model groups. The
forced one-pixel GIF fallback completed and fetched omggif (32,016 body bytes). The explicit
depth-loader probe created an inference session and fetched the runtime entry (50,126 bytes), model
(27,258,801 bytes), WASM helper (24,381 bytes), and WASM binary (14,239,897 bytes). This checks
loading, not a full photo conversion or inference result. A separate sound-enabled Dice probe
fetched its 4,614-byte sample; the default matrix keeps sound off. Exact URLs, request headers,
timing entries, and probe results are in `site-probes.json`.

These HEAD responses were fetched on October 2, 2026 (Eastern time), with Accept-Encoding: gzip;
their timestamps and headers are in `cache-headers.json`. All successful samples returned
max-age=600 and validation metadata. Local payload ranks intentionally omit that live
compression/cache behavior. Reusing unchanged resources within the current cache lifetime avoids
repeated bodies; longer cache behavior or a future hosting change is untested. The table uses
successful HEAD observations; complete request receipts are retained in the data.

## Method and limits

The frozen catalog has 342 entries: 32 captured, 306 kit, and 4 procedural. The Python server serves
the unchanged baseline source at `http://127.0.0.1:4173`; only the audit output differs from main.
Chrome is `154.0.8037.95`, with ANGLE Metal on the Apple M3 Pro. The phone context is 390×844,
device scale 3, touch/mobile enabled, `profile=weak` (alias `low`); the desktop is 1440×900, device
scale 1, `profile=high`. Both force WebGL2 and `adapt=off`. The low tier’s default canvas cap is
1.5; a recipe can override it (`src/player.js:50`); viewport size is not GPU resolution. Each row
saves its actual canvas size.

At the final check, origin/main was `bc1fc5405b8e`. The app, assets, vendor libraries, and tests
matched the measured baseline; the intervening changes were confined to operating notes and a
separate effect-review page.

Each toy opens in a fresh context through an uncompressed scene link, using its default
look/options, fixed scene seed 424242, preset camera, and the gallery’s still-toy turntable choice.
Cache is disabled, sound is off, reduced motion is off, and the network is unthrottled localhost.
Main-thread sampling never overlaps another toy. Browser/OS caches and other Mac applications are
not controlled; a single run per cell is a screening measurement, not a statistically stable device
benchmark.

Readiness follows `body[data-ready=true]` (`tests/smoke.spec.mjs:31`, `src/app.js:200`) and the
requested `player.toyInfo.id`. “First frame” means the first submitted `postrender` after that
readiness state. It is not a GPU fence or pixel/paint confirmation, and a PDF page or image can
finish streaming later. The 1.5-second settling delay follows the smoke helper
(`tests/smoke.spec.mjs:40`). Table counts include the settled base and picture splats, plus
separately verified rig add-ons (`src/player.js:562`, `src/stage.js:384`). Initial and post-tap
base/picture counts are also saved. Fluid lab adds its allocated fluid-layer capacity, which can
include inactive slots; its active particles are recorded separately. The supplemental census uses
the same defaults and seed, after settling, outside the timing pass. Table JS heap is CDP
`Runtime.getHeapUsage.usedSize` after the opening settling delay, without forced garbage collection;
the immediate first-ready snapshot is also saved. Backing storage and both snapshots are in the
data, but worker/GPU/native memory is excluded. This distinction follows
[Chrome’s memory documentation](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/devtools/memory-problems).

Opening MiB is HTTP body payload completed before the idle window starts, after settling. Completed
page requests use the observed Content-Length; worker script bodies absent from page CDP completion
events use the parent’s Resource Timing encodedBodySize, and worker imports/font downloads use each
worker’s own Resource Timing. Same-origin timing entries with zero transferSize are treated as
cached bodies. Worker timing can be zero/negative, so its cutoff is approximate; the observed byte
count is retained. These are local uncompressed payloads, not live compressed-wire totals. CDP
encodedDataLength, timestamps, Resource Timing transfer/body sizes, and the first-frame cutoff are
separately retained in `network.csv`. Blob/data URLs are excluded. Uncompleted/aborted response
bodies are excluded, even when some bytes might have arrived; the Content-Length upper bound is
saved. HTTP headers are excluded from the table metric, matching the body-byte convention in
`tests/smoke.spec.mjs:381`.

There are five seconds of natural idle, then the app’s default Play/tap action (`app.act()`, the
same null-point action the browser tests use), then five seconds after the tap. This does not choose
every key, chess square, or option. Frame times are wall-clock intervals between PlayCanvas
`frameend` events, with the ordinary sample median (averaging the two middle values when even) and
nearest-rank 95th percentile. Those events include update-only loops: rendering is on demand
(`src/stage.js:81`, `src/stage.js:110`). Render counts distinguish a still toy from a continuously
drawn one. These numbers are event cadence, not isolated CPU render time or GPU duration. Actual
elapsed windows and every interval are saved; no time dilation or synthetic advancement is used.
Both frameend and postrender interval vectors are saved. Fluid lab runs the automated-browser sync
solver (`src/fluids/runtime.js:24`); normal visitors can use a worker, and WebGPU uses a different
path. Sound-enabled and live-input behavior need separate probes.

## Verification, unknowns, and reproduction

The audit changes only this report and its CSV/JSON data directory. `methodology.json` stores the
complete collector source, launch flags, runtime/device definition, baseline, metric definitions,
and final verification receipts. `measurements.json` stores the full matrix; `frame-samples.csv`
stores every frame-end and postrender interval, with toy/setting/window/event groups mapped in
`frame-series.json`; `network.csv` stores request and Resource Timing observations.
`static-inventory.json` records file sizes and SOG metadata counts, and `recipes.json` records the
inspected default recipe metadata. The gallery and lazy-load probes have their own JSON receipts.
`lossless-ply-check.json` records the temporary normal-column removal and decoded/framed/pruned
equality check; it does not edit an asset. Preflight attempts are excluded from the committed
matrix.

Validation confirmed all 684 cells and 1,368 five-second windows, zero page/console/worker-timing
errors, and exact preservation of the collector interval vectors. Prettier for the whole checkout
and the report, US English diff checking, and staged whitespace checking passed. The named
embedded-capture transfer test passed with the existing SwiftShader test configuration, reporting
8.6 MiB (rounded); the timing matrix uses ANGLE Metal. Two aborted duplicate Screen video requests
have the bounds reported above.

To reproduce, extract `collectorSource` from methodology.json into a temporary `.mjs` file, update
its absolute checkout paths, and run it with the saved Chrome executable and a Python server on
port 4173. Its five-second real-time windows make the full matrix take at least 114 minutes, plus
loading and settling. Run the saved finalizer source to regenerate the derived payload/count records
and CSV files. Collect only one rendering page at a time. Inspect actual GPU/profile/canvas and
status fields before comparing a rerun.

Every requested toy/setting completed the two sampling windows. Physical-device acceptance,
energy/thermal measurements, all options/looks, all point-specific taps, enabled-sound performance,
and proposed fidelity reductions remain unverified. A first-ready submitted frame does not confirm
that a streamed page is already legible. Heap observations do not establish leaks or full process
memory, and small timing rank differences can be noise.
